  "description": "Get aggregated check states per PR"
}

### 🟡 Failing — trivial fix (1)
**[#336](https://github.com/TheurgicDuke771/DataQ/pull/336) sqlalchemy 2.0.50→2.0.51** — only failure is the `typecheck-deps-sync` gate. Dependabot bumped `requirements-typecheck.txt` (`sqlalchemy[mypy]==2.0.51`) but not the matching pin in `.pre-commit-config.yaml`'s mypy hook (still `2.0.50`). One-line fix to the pre-commit hook, then it's green. This is exactly the drift the CLAUDE.md gate is designed to catch.

### 🔴 Blocked — real conflict (1)
**[#338](https://github.com/TheurgicDuke771/DataQ/pull/338) redis 5.2.1→8.0.0** — hard dependency conflict, not a flake:
```
Cannot install kombu[redis]==5.6.x and redis==8.0.0 — conflicting dependencies
```
Celery's `kombu[redis]` extra caps `redis` below 8. This bump can't merge until Celery/kombu support redis 8. Recommend **closing** it (Dependabot will re-open if/when the constraint lifts), or leaving a note and waiting.

---

**Suggested actions:** merge the 9 green ones; let me fix #336's pre-commit pin (quick fixup commit); close #338 with a comment. Want me to do any of those? I'd recommend at minimum fixing #336 and closing #338.

Let me check whether forcing it is even viable, and whether there's any security pressure behind the bump.

> AGENT

## Verdict: technically forceable, but you shouldn't — and there's no reason to

**What's actually blocking it:** The cap is `redis<6.5`, declared by the `[redis]` extra in **kombu** (Celery's broker library), pulled in via `celery[redis]`. I checked PyPI — the *latest* kombu (5.6.2) and *latest* celery (5.6.3) both still declare that cap. There is **no released celery/kombu that allows redis 8.x.** redis-py renumbered to 8.0 to align with the Redis 8 server, and Celery hasn't adopted that scheme yet.

**Could we force it?** Yes — drop the `[redis]` extra and pin redis directly:
```
celery==5.6.3        # was celery[redis]==5.6.3
redis==8.0.1
```
Plain `kombu` (no extra) doesn't constrain redis, so the resolver conflict disappears and it *installs*. **But** that just silences the resolver — it overrides the maintainer's tested ceiling. Kombu's redis broker transport drives redis-py internals; on 8.x it's untested upstream and could break the Celery broker at runtime. CI would go green on install/unit tests and we'd be blind on actual broker behavior (which our test suite mocks).

**Is there any pressure to do this?** No. I checked OSV: **redis 5.2.1 has zero known vulnerabilities.** This PR is pure Dependabot version-chasing, not a security fix.

## Recommendation
Don't force it. The cost (overriding a tested constraint + needing real broker integration testing) buys nothing — there's no CVE, and redis 8.x gains us no feature we use. **Close #338** and let Dependabot re-propose once Celery/kombu officially support redis 8.

I'd also suggest adding a Dependabot `ignore` for `redis` major bumps so it stops re-opening this every release until upstream catches up.

Want me to:
1. **Close #338** with a comment explaining the kombu cap (recommended), and
2. Add the `ignore: redis [version-update:semver-major]` rule to `.github/dependabot.yml`?

> DEVELOPER

yeah, go with it

> AGENT

I'll do both. Let me close #338 with an explanatory comment and add the Dependabot ignore rule.

**#338 closed** — with a comment explaining the upstream cap (kombu's `[redis]` extra pins `redis<6.5`, no released celery/kombu allows 8.x) and that redis 5.2.1 has no CVEs, so there's no reason to force it. Remote branch deleted.

Patch/minor redis updates still flow; only majors are suppressed until Celery/kombu adopt the Redis-8-aligned versioning.

════════════════════ ISSUE #8 ════════════════════
TITLE: GitHub templates: title prefixes, multi-select wording, screenshots section
CREATED: 2026-05-24T07:40:35Z

Follow-up from `/review` on PR #3. Non-blocking polish to land in a follow-up `chore:` PR.

## Items

- [ ] **Issue title prefixes use PR-side conventional types.** `bug.md` has `title: "fix: <short description>"` and `feature.md` has `title: "feat: <short description>"`. `fix:` / `feat:` are PR/commit prefixes describing a *change*; issues describe a *problem* or *request*. Switch to plain descriptive titles or use issue-side prefixes (e.g. `[bug]`).
- [ ] **PR template "Type of change" is multi-select but conventional commits are singular.** Wording says "Tick all that apply" — squash-merge produces one commit with one type prefix. Change to "Tick **one** that matches your conventional commit prefix."
- [ ] **PR template `Fixes #` pre-fill leaves a literal half-token.** If author doesn't fill or delete, GitHub doesn't parse it. Leave blank with comment hint, or use `Fixes #N` (literal `N`) so reviewers spot the placeholder.
- [ ] **Schema-migration question rendered as checkboxes** but options are mutually exclusive. Add "Tick exactly one." for clarity.
- [ ] **PR template missing screenshots/video section.** Add `## UI evidence (frontend changes only)` now so the habit is established before Week 4 frontend work.
- [ ] **PR template checklist references tools not yet installed.** `Black + Ruff + mypy pass locally` / `Prettier + ESLint pass locally` — add `(from Week 1 onward)` suffix like the testing line.

TITLE: ADR template improvements: Consulted, Supersedes fields; clarify commit-type subset; fix untyped dict
CREATED: 2026-05-24T07:41:47Z

Follow-up from `/review` on PR #4. Non-blocking polish to land in a follow-up `docs:` PR.

## Items

- [ ] **ADR 0001 branch-name list doesn't match ADR 0002 commit-type list.** ADR 0001 lists `feature/`, `fix/`, `chore/`, `docs/`. ADR 0002 also allows `test:` and `refactor:`. Either add `refactor/` and `test/` to ADR 0001, or state "branch prefix matches the conventional-commit type of the eventual PR title."
- [ ] **ADR 0002 restricts conventional commits without saying so.** Spec also defines `build:`, `ci:`, `perf:`, `style:`, `revert:`. State the restriction is intentional and why.
- [ ] **ADR 0002 example contradicts ADR 0003 pinning policy.** Example shows `chore: pin GX Core to 1.x` (range); ADR 0003 says "pin the version" (exact). Update example to `chore: pin GX Core to 1.4.2` or similar.
- [ ] **ADR 0004 interface uses untyped `dict`.** `headers: dict` sets a poor type-hint example. Use `Mapping[str, str]` or `dict[str, str]`.
- [ ] **ADR 0004 doesn't address retry/backoff for polling.** Add a line: "retry/backoff is implementation detail; interface guarantees no double-emit via `last_updated_at` dedupe."
- [ ] **ADR template missing `Consulted` / `Stakeholders` field.** ADR 0005 (severity tier weights) needs product-owner sign-off per the plan. Add a frontmatter slot.
- [ ] **ADR template missing explicit `Supersedes` / `Superseded by` placeholders.** README describes the supersession process but template doesn't reserve a slot.
- [ ] **Write ADR 0009 (repo layout)** to resolve the Pending/Locked status drift with CLAUDE.md §9.

════════════════════ ISSUE #18 ════════════════════
TITLE: Hooks polish: $schema verify, .envrc decision, per-tool matchers
CREATED: 2026-05-24T08:11:08Z

Follow-up from `/review` on PR #14. Non-blocking polish.

## Items

- [ ] **Verify `$schema` URL resolves.** `.claude/settings.json` references `https://json.schemastore.org/claude-code-settings.json`. If schemastore doesn't have this entry, the URL is a 404 and IDE autocomplete won't work — drop the `$schema` line in that case.
- [ ] **Decide on `.envrc` (direnv) blocking.** Current regex `(^|/)\.env($|\.)` matches `.env`, `.env.local`, etc., but NOT `.envrc`. direnv files can contain secrets and aren't currently `.gitignore`d. Either extend the regex to `(^|/)\.env($|\.|rc$)` or add `.envrc` to `.gitignore`.
- [ ] **Consider per-tool matchers.** Currently `Write|Edit|MultiEdit` fires the Python autoformat for every text edit (Markdown, JSON, TOML), forking a shell to no-op. Minor performance; not urgent.

`/review` comment: https://github.com/TheurgicDuke771/DataQ/pull/14#issuecomment-4527783623


════════════════════ ISSUE #19 ════════════════════
TITLE: Claude skills polish: helper script, status case, --severity wiring, examples
CREATED: 2026-05-24T08:11:11Z

Follow-up from `/review` on PR #15. Non-blocking polish.

## Items

### adr-create
- [ ] **Bundle a `next-number.sh` helper script** alongside `SKILL.md` so the next-ADR-number detection is deterministic instead of LLM-re-inferred each call. Suggested:
  ```bash
  #!/usr/bin/env bash
  last=$(ls docs/adr/[0-9][0-9][0-9][0-9]-*.md 2>/dev/null | sed -E 's|.*/([0-9]+)-.*|\1|' | sort -n | tail -1)
  printf '%04d\n' $(( 10#${last:-0} + 1 ))
  ```
- [ ] **Normalize status case.** CLI arg is lowercase (`accepted|proposed|superseded`); template defaults to `Accepted` (title-case). Always render title-case in the file.
- [ ] **Add `Deprecated` as a valid status.** ADRs sometimes get deprecated without being superseded.
- [ ] **Add a "Test scenarios" section** with worked examples so Claude executes consistently.

### gh-issue-from-finding
- [ ] **Use or drop `--severity`.** The flag is declared with detailed guidance but never referenced by Steps or Body template. Either add `**Severity:** <value>` to the body, or map to a `priority:*` label, or drop the flag.
- [ ] **Validate `--type` against existing labels** before calling `gh issue create`. Currently passing `--type custom` would fail with "label not found" — either pre-validate or state the constraint in the skill doc.
- [ ] **Add a "Test scenarios" section** with worked examples.

1	# ADR 0001 — Trunk-based branching with squash-merge into `main`
2	
3	- **Status:** Accepted
4	- **Date:** 2026-05-24
5	- **Deciders:** @TheurgicDuke771
6	
7	## Context
8	
9	DataQ v1 has an 8-week timeline and a small team (starting solo, expected to grow). We need a branching model that supports frequent integration, keeps `main` history readable, and aligns with the working-agreement of one functionality per commit.
10	
11	## Decision
12	
13	Adopt **trunk-based development** with short-lived feature branches off `main`, and **squash-merge** as the only allowed merge method into `main`.
14	
15	- All work happens on a branch named `feature/<desc>`, `fix/issue-<N>-<desc>`, `chore/<desc>`, or `docs/<desc>`.
16	- Branches are short-lived (ideally hours to a couple of days), opened as PRs against `main`.
17	- PRs are squash-merged — the PR title becomes the single commit on `main` (so PR titles MUST follow conventional commits — see ADR 0002).
18	- No long-lived `develop` branch.
19	- `main` is protected via a GitHub ruleset (deletion blocked, force-push blocked, linear history required, PR required, squash-only merge, dismiss stale reviews on new push). Admin (sole maintainer) can bypass in emergencies.
20	- Auto-delete head branches on merge.
21	
22	## Consequences
23	
24	**Positive**
25	- `main` history reads as one commit per delivered functionality — matches working-agreement #1.
26	- Short-lived branches force small, reviewable changes.
27	- No long-lived branches to keep in sync.
28	- Linear history makes `git bisect` reliable.
29	
30	**Negative**
31	- Loses individual WIP commits inside a PR. Mitigated: those commits remain visible in the PR view on GitHub.
32	- Solo dev currently has `required_approving_review_count: 0` — must bump to 1 when a second contributor joins (tracked as a future change).
33	
34	## Alternatives considered
35	
36	- **GitFlow** — rejected. The `develop` / `release` / `hotfix` overhead is unjustified for an 8-week single-tenant v1; release cadence is continuous, not versioned.
37	- **Merge commits preserving branch history** — rejected. Squash gives one commit per functionality on `main`; preserved branch history would dilute that signal and clutter `git log`.
38	- **Rebase-and-merge** — rejected. Preserves all WIP commits on `main`, breaking the one-functionality-per-commit principle.
39	
```

1	# ADR 0002 — Conventional commits for PR titles and commit messages
2	
3	- **Status:** Accepted
4	- **Date:** 2026-05-24
5	- **Deciders:** @TheurgicDuke771
6	
7	## Context
8	
9	With squash-merge as the only allowed merge method (ADR 0001), each PR becomes exactly one commit on `main` whose message is the PR title. The PR title format therefore drives the readability of `git log` and enables future automation (changelog generation, release notes).
10	
11	## Decision
12	
13	Use **Conventional Commits** for all PR titles and individual commits.
14	
15	Allowed type prefixes:
16	
17	| Type | Use for |
18	|---|---|
19	| `feat:` | New user-facing functionality |
20	| `fix:` | Bug fix (PR body MUST include `Fixes #N` per working-agreement #3) |
21	| `chore:` | Tooling, dependencies, repo housekeeping |
22	| `docs:` | Documentation-only change (CLAUDE.md, ADRs, README, code comments) |
23	| `test:` | Test-only change |
24	| `refactor:` | Internal change with no behaviour change |
25	
26	Format: `<type>(<optional-scope>): <imperative summary>`
27	
28	Examples:
29	- `feat(orchestration): add Airflow callback webhook handler`
30	- `fix(adf): debounce duplicate trigger events within 30s window`
31	- `chore: pin GX Core to 1.x`
32	- `docs: add ADR 0005 for severity tier weights`
33	
34	## Consequences
35	
36	**Positive**
37	- `git log --oneline` reads as a categorised changelog.
38	- Enables future tooling: auto-changelog, release-please, semantic versioning.
39	- PR titles are self-documenting.
40	
41	**Negative**
42	- One more thing for new contributors to learn. Mitigated: the PR template includes a "Type of change" checklist that nudges the right prefix.
43	
44	## Enforcement
45	
46	- PR template includes the convention.
47	- Future enhancement (post-W1): a GitHub Action that fails CI if the PR title does not match the convention.
48	
```

The spec also defines `build:`, `ci:`, `perf:`, `style:`, and `revert:`; we deliberately exclude them to keep the `git log` taxonomy small and unambiguous for a small team — build/CI changes ride `chore:`, performance work rides `fix:` or `refactor:`, formatting rides `chore:`/`refactor:`, and reverts use `revert:`-style PR titles only if the spec type is later adopted. Expand the table if a real need emerges.\n\nFormat: `<type>(<optional-scope>): <imperative summary>`"
}

1	# ADR 0004 — Unified `OrchestrationProvider` abstraction for ADF and Apache Airflow
2	
3	- **Status:** Accepted
4	- **Date:** 2026-05-24
5	- **Deciders:** @TheurgicDuke771
6	
7	## Context
8	
9	DataQ integrates with Azure Data Factory (ADF) and Apache Airflow. The original product framing listed ADF as a "5th datasource type," which conflates orchestration with data storage:
10	
11	- **Datasources** (Snowflake, ADLS Gen2, S3, Unity Catalog) are stores against which DQ checks are written.
12	- **Orchestrators** (ADF, Airflow) are workflow engines whose pipeline/DAG runs we observe and react to. We do not write DQ checks against the orchestrator itself.
13	
14	Treating orchestrators as datasources pollutes the suite/check editor, mixes unrelated concerns in the data model, and makes the upcoming Airflow integration look like a one-off rather than a sibling of ADF.
15	
16	## Decision
17	
18	**Treat ADF and Apache Airflow as orchestration integrations behind a single `OrchestrationProvider` abstraction. They are NOT datasources and never appear in the connection editor / check editor / suite model as a queryable target.**
19	
20	### Responsibilities (identical across both providers)
21	
22	1. **Monitor** pipeline/DAG runs → stored in `pipeline_runs` table with a `provider` column (`adf` | `airflow`) and `provider_run_id`.
23	2. **Detect failure** in near-real-time via provider-specific webhook channels (see table below).
24	3. **Trigger suite execution on successful completion** via `trigger_bindings`. The table has a surrogate primary key (`id`) and a non-unique composite **lookup index** on (`provider`, `pipeline_or_dag_id`, `env`) — one pipeline/DAG can map to many suites (one row per binding). Failure events alert the user but do NOT trigger suite runs (no point running checks on data that wasn't loaded).
25	
26	### Interface
27	
28	```
29	class OrchestrationProvider:
30	    def parse_event(self, payload: bytes, headers: dict) -> RunUpdate: ...
31	    def fetch_run_detail(self, provider_run_id: str) -> RunDetail: ...
32	    def list_recent_runs(self, since: datetime) -> list[RunUpdate]: ...
33	```
34	
35	Both `AdfProvider` and `AirflowProvider` implement this interface. Service code routes by `provider` enum, never by provider-specific branching.
36	
37	### Event channels per provider
38	
39	| Provider | Failure / success channel | Auth | Polling fallback |
40	|---|---|---|---|
41	| ADF | Azure Monitor alert rule → webhook `POST /api/v1/orchestration/events/adf` | Shared-secret header (Azure Monitor's only auth mode) | ADF REST API every 10 min |
42	| Airflow | DAG `on_success_callback` / `on_failure_callback` → webhook `POST /api/v1/orchestration/events/airflow` | HMAC-signed payload (signing key in Key Vault) | Airflow REST API `dagRuns` every 10 min |
43	
44	### Single-orchestrator-per-`(provider, env)` assumption
45	
46	`trigger_bindings` is keyed by (`provider`, `pipeline_or_dag_id`, `env`) → `suite_id`. This composite key **cannot disambiguate two instances of the same provider in the same environment** — e.g. two separate `airflow` deployments both serving `env=dev`. The agreed v1 shape therefore assumes **exactly one orchestrator instance per (`provider`, `env`) pair**.
47	
48	**Consequence:** to keep the binding lookup unambiguous, one of two things must hold:
49	
50	1. Connection-level CRUD enforces **uniqueness on (`type`, `env`) for orchestrator-typed connections** (an `adf`/`airflow` connection is singular per env), OR
51	2. `trigger_bindings` grows a `connection_id` column so a binding names a specific orchestrator instance.
52	
53	**v1 decision:** take option 1 — document the assumption (this section) and **enforce `(type, env)` uniqueness at the connection-CRUD layer in Week 2** for orchestrator-typed connections. The schema bump (option 2) is **deferred to v1.1** and only revisited if multi-instance-per-env becomes a real requirement. Without this guard, a future contributor adding a second same-env Airflow connection would hit a silent binding collision rather than a validation error.
54	
55	### UI surface
56	
57	Orchestrators appear ONLY in:
58	- The orchestration status panel on the results dashboard.
59	- The trigger-binding configuration UI.
60	
61	They do NOT appear in the connection editor's "pick a datasource" list or the check editor's table picker.
62	
63	## Consequences
64	
65	**Positive**
66	- Adding a third orchestration provider in the future (Prefect, Dagster, etc.) is a new `OrchestrationProvider` implementation — no data-model or UI churn.
67	- `pipeline_runs` stays clean as a single events stream regardless of provider.
68	- `trigger_bindings` is provider-agnostic from day one.
69	- Forces correct mental model — orchestrator is not a datasource.
70	
71	**Negative**
72	- Slightly more abstraction upfront in Week 2 vs hardcoding ADF, then refactoring when Airflow is added. Accepted because Airflow is in-scope for v1, not a hypothetical future.
73	- Airflow callback adoption is voluntary on the user side (we cannot mutate their DAGs). Mitigated by documenting the polling fallback and providing a copy-paste `dataq_airflow_callback` snippet.
74	
75	## Alternatives considered
76	
77	- **Treat ADF as a 5th datasource (original framing)** — rejected. Conflates orchestration with storage; pollutes the check editor and the data model.
78	- **Hardcode ADF in v1, abstract later when Airflow is added** — rejected. Refactoring after `pipeline_runs` has data is more expensive than designing the abstraction once upfront.
79	
80	## Related
81	
82	- `pipeline_runs` and `trigger_bindings` schema lands in Week 1/2.
83	- `OrchestrationProvider` interface lands in Week 2 alongside both implementations.
84	- The single-orchestrator-per-`(provider, env)` assumption (above) was surfaced in [PR #41](https://github.com/TheurgicDuke771/DataQ/pull/41) review and tracked in [#72](https://github.com/TheurgicDuke771/DataQ/issues/72); the `(type, env)` uniqueness guard is a Week 2 connection-CRUD requirement.
85	- Future: ADR 0006 (ADF webhook auth) and ADR 0007 (Airflow callback model) will detail the provider-specific event channels.
86	
```

  "old_string": "```\nclass OrchestrationProvider:\n    def parse_event(self, payload: bytes, headers: dict) -> RunUpdate: ...\n    def fetch_run_detail(self, provider_run_id: str) -> RunDetail: ...\n    def list_recent_runs(self, since: datetime) -> list[RunUpdate]: ...\n```\n\nBoth `AdfProvider` and `AirflowProvider` implement this interface. Service code routes by `provider` enum, never by provider-specific branching.",
  "new_string": "```\nfrom collections.abc import Mapping\n\nclass OrchestrationProvider:\n    def parse_event(self, payload: bytes, headers: Mapping[str, str]) -> RunUpdate: ...\n    def fetch_run_detail(self, provider_run_id: str) -> RunDetail: ...\n    def list_recent_runs(self, since: datetime) -> list[RunUpdate]: ...\n```\n\nBoth `AdfProvider` and `AirflowProvider` implement this interface. Service code routes by `provider` enum, never by provider-specific branching.\n\n**Retry/backoff is an implementation detail**, not part of this interface. The polling fallback (`list_recent_runs`) may retry with backoff on transient provider-API errors; the interface guarantees no double-emit because ingestion is idempotent on (`provider`, `provider_run_id`) plus the run's `last_updated_at` — a re-delivered or re-polled event for an already-current run is a no-op upsert, not a duplicate `pipeline_runs` row."
}
```

1	# Architecture Decision Records (ADRs)
2	
3	Each ADR captures a single significant architecture decision: the context, the decision, the consequences, and the alternatives considered. New ADRs are append-only — supersede an old decision by adding a new ADR and marking the old one's status as `Superseded by ADR-NNNN`.
4	
5	## Format
6	
7	- File name: `NNNN-short-kebab-slug.md` (zero-padded 4-digit sequence)
8	- Frontmatter fields: Status, Date, Deciders
9	- Sections: Context, Decision, Consequences, Alternatives considered, Related (optional)
10	- Keep each ADR short — 1–2 pages. If it grows past that, the decision is probably two decisions.
11	
12	## Index
13	
14	| # | Title | Status |
15	|---|---|---|
16	| [0001](0001-trunk-based-branching.md) | Trunk-based branching with squash-merge into `main` | Accepted |
17	| [0002](0002-conventional-commits.md) | Conventional commits for PR titles and commit messages | Accepted |
18	| [0003](0003-gx-only-for-v1.md) | GX-only for v1; DQX deferred to v1.1 for DLT/streaming | Accepted |
19	| [0004](0004-orchestration-abstraction.md) | Unified `OrchestrationProvider` abstraction for ADF and Airflow | Accepted |
20	| [0005](0005-severity-tier-weights.md) | Severity tier weights (warn / fail / critical → health score) | Accepted |
21	| [0006](0006-adf-webhook-authentication.md) | ADF webhook authentication (shared secret in URL + hard-cutover rotation) | Accepted |
22	| [0007](0007-airflow-callback-model.md) | Airflow callback model (HMAC-signed webhook + polling fallback) | Accepted |
23	| [0009](0009-flat-monorepo-layout.md) | Repo layout — flat monorepo (`backend/` + `frontend/`) | Accepted |
24	| [0010](0010-provider-agnostic-infrastructure-seams.md) | Provider-agnostic infrastructure seams (Azure is the default, not the architecture) | Accepted |
25	| [0011](0011-extensibility-seams-for-deferred-integrations.md) | Extensibility seams for deferred connectors and integrations | Accepted |
26	| [0012](0012-monitor-kind-seam.md) | Monitor-kind seam (`check.kind` discriminator + numeric metric storage) | Accepted |
27	| [0013](0013-marketplace-distribution-and-anti-lock-in.md) | Marketplace distribution (customer-deployed BYOL) and anti-vendor-lock-in guardrails | Accepted |
28	| [0014](0014-reconciliation-comparison-check-kind.md) | Cross-dataset reconciliation as a `comparison` check kind (reuse FastAPI_DataComparison engine) | Accepted |
29	| [0016](0016-severity-derivation-semantics.md) | Severity derivation semantics (band the unexpected-%, thresholds override GX success) | Accepted |
30	| [0017](0017-python-313-runtime-upgrade.md) | Upgrade Python runtime 3.11 → 3.13 (3.14 deferred — GX-capped); bundled with the Snowflake 3→4 CVE refresh | Accepted |
31	| [0018](0018-results-surface-and-grafana-deferral.md) | Results surface is an in-app page (suite-scoped authz + PII redaction); Grafana deferred to optional ops add-on | Accepted |
32	| [0019](0019-custom-sql-check-kind.md) | Custom-SQL checks ride `kind='expectation'` via GX `UnexpectedRowsExpectation` (no new kind); read-only validation + SQL-datasource gating | Accepted |
33	| [0020](0020-history-and-audit-strategy.md) | History/audit: per-entity Type-4 snapshot tables (`check_versions`, `connection_versions`) where config history is needed; no SCD-2; credentials never snapshotted; cascade-delete accepted; cross-entity audit log deferred | Accepted |
34	| [0021](0021-demo-test-data-environment-strategy.md) | Live test/demo-data environment (retail model, 3 reference flows) lives outside the repo — Terraform/mock-data/Databricks notebook not git-tracked; discharges the deferred live-warehouse/file smoke | Accepted |
35	| [0022](0022-week6-prototype-adoption-and-chart-library.md) | Week-6 prototype adoption — full 13-screen set as dedicated pages (Share is the only drawer; prototype wins on conflicts; Settings/Admin pulled into W6); chart library = recharts (lazy-loaded) | Accepted |
36	
37	## Pending (to be written in their respective weeks)
38	
39	| # | Topic | Target week |
40	|---|---|---|
41	| 0008 | MCP mounted at `/mcp` with Azure AD auth | Week 7 |
42	| 0015 | Two-connection check model (source + target refs for `comparison` checks) | When reconciliation build starts (post-v1) |
43

Add when the status flips to `Superseded by`.\n- Sections: Context, Decision, Consequences, Alternatives considered, Related (optional)\n- Keep each ADR short — 1–2 pages. If it grows past that, the decision is probably two decisions.\n\n### Template\n\n```markdown\n# ADR NNNN — <title>\n\n- **Status:** Proposed\n- **Date:** YYYY-MM-DD\n- **Deciders:** @handle\n- **Consulted:** <stakeholder>   <!-- optional; omit when none -->\n- **Supersedes:** ADR-NNNN       <!-- optional; omit when none -->\n- **Superseded by:** ADR-NNNN    <!-- optional; add when superseded -->\n\n## Context\n\n## Decision\n\n## Consequences\n\n## Alternatives considered\n```"

1	---
2	name: adr-create
3	description: Scaffold a new Architecture Decision Record under docs/adr/ with auto-numbered filename, standard frontmatter (Status / Date / Deciders / optional Consulted / Supersedes / Superseded by), and the canonical sections (Context, Decision, Consequences, Alternatives considered, Related). Use when the user wants to capture a significant architectural decision, or asks "let's ADR that."
4	disable-model-invocation: true
5	---
6	
7	# adr-create
8	
9	## Purpose
10	
11	Create a new ADR in `docs/adr/` that follows the conventions documented in `docs/adr/README.md` and is consistent with the ADRs already in the repo.
12	
13	## Usage
14	
15	User invokes this skill with a short topic for the ADR. Optional args:
16	- `--status accepted|proposed|superseded` (default: `Accepted`)
17	- `--consulted "@user1, @user2"` (omit if engineering-only decision)
18	- `--supersedes 0007` (omit if not superseding)
19	
20	## Steps
21	
22	1. **Determine the next ADR number.** Read existing files in `docs/adr/` matching `NNNN-*.md`, find the highest number, increment by 1, zero-pad to 4 digits. If `docs/adr/` doesn't exist, start at `0001`.
23	
24	2. **Derive a kebab-case slug** from the user's topic. Drop articles. Examples:
25	   - "Severity tier weights" → `severity-tier-weights`
26	   - "Use Pydantic Settings for config" → `use-pydantic-settings-for-config`
27	
28	3. **Create the file** at `docs/adr/NNNN-<slug>.md` using the template below. Today's date in ISO format (`YYYY-MM-DD`). Deciders defaults to the current GitHub handle, resolved via `gh api /user --jq .login` (prepend `@`). Do NOT use `git config user.name` — that returns a display name (e.g. "Arijit Roy") which produces an invalid `@mention`. If `gh api` fails (no auth, offline), fall back to asking the user for their GitHub handle.
29	
30	4. **Update the index** at `docs/adr/README.md`:
31	   - Add a row to the "Index" table at the end (above any "Pending" section).
32	   - If the new ADR was in the Pending list, remove it from there.
33	
34	5. **Stage but do not commit.** The user runs `git add` + the commit themselves per working-agreement #1 (one functionality per commit, manual commit step).
35	
36	6. **Print next steps:**
37	   - "Open `docs/adr/NNNN-<slug>.md` and fill in Context / Decision / Consequences / Alternatives."
38	   - "Branch suggestion: `docs/adr-NNNN-<slug>`"
39	   - "PR title suggestion: `docs: add ADR NNNN — <human title>`"
40	
41	## Template
42	
43	```markdown
44	# ADR NNNN — <Human-readable title>
45	
46	- **Status:** Accepted
47	- **Date:** YYYY-MM-DD
48	- **Deciders:** @<github-handle>
49	- **Consulted:** <optional, e.g. @product-owner, @security-team>
50	- **Supersedes:** <optional, e.g. ADR-0007>
51	- **Superseded by:** <optional, e.g. ADR-0042>
52	
53	## Context
54	
55	<What problem are we deciding about? What forces are at play (constraints, requirements, prior decisions)? Keep to 1–3 paragraphs.>
56	
57	## Decision
58	
59	**<One-sentence statement of the decision, in bold.>**
60	
61	<Then the details: how it works, what's in scope, what's out of scope.>
62	
63	## Consequences
64	
65	**Positive**
66	- <bullet>
67	- <bullet>
68	
69	**Negative**
70	- <bullet>
71	- <bullet>
72	
73	## Alternatives considered
74	
75	- **<Alternative 1>** — rejected. <Why.>
76	- **<Alternative 2>** — rejected. <Why.>
77	
78	## Related
79	
80	- <Link to related ADRs, issues, PRs, or code paths.>
81	```
82	
83	## Rules
84	
85	- **Status field is required.** Default to `Accepted` unless `--status` says otherwise.
86	- **Date is today's date** in ISO format.
87	- **Consulted, Supersedes, Superseded by are optional** — include the label even if empty so future editors see the slot.
88	- **Body sections in this exact order:** Context → Decision → Consequences (with Positive/Negative subheadings) → Alternatives considered → Related.
89	- **Slug must match the convention** in `docs/adr/README.md`: lowercase, kebab-case, no leading article.
90	- **Filename matches title:** `NNNN-<slug>.md` where the slug is also derivable from the H1 title.
91	- **README index is the source of truth** for which ADRs exist. Always update it.
92	- **Do not commit.** Stage only. The user commits manually so they can review the diff.
93	
94	## Anti-patterns to avoid
95	
96	- Don't reuse a number. If `docs/adr/0005-*.md` exists, the next one is `0006`, never `0005a` or `0005-v2`.
97	- Don't auto-fill Decision/Context with boilerplate. Leave them as the template placeholders so the user must write the actual content.
98	- Don't add the ADR to the "Pending" section of README — Pending is for ADRs known to be coming in a specific week but not yet written.
99	
```

{
  "file_path": "/Users/arijit/Coding/Python/DataQ/.claude/skills/adr-create/next-number.sh",
  "content": "#!/usr/bin/env bash\n# Print the next zero-padded 4-digit ADR number for docs/adr/.\n# Deterministic helper so the next-number is not re-inferred by the model each call.\n# Usage: .claude/skills/adr-create/next-number.sh [adr-dir]\n#   adr-dir defaults to docs/adr (relative to repo root / CWD).\nset -euo pipefail\n\ndir=\"${1:-docs/adr}\"\n\n# Highest existing NNNN prefix among NNNN-*.md files; 0 when the dir is empty/missing.\nlast=$(ls \"$dir\"/[0-9][0-9][0-9][0-9]-*.md 2>/dev/null \\\n  | sed -E 's|.*/([0-9]{4})-.*|\\1|' \\\n  | sort -n \\\n  | tail -1)\n\nprintf '%04d\\n' $(( 10#${last:-0} + 1 ))\n"
}

1	---
2	name: gh-issue-from-finding
3	description: Create a properly-formatted GitHub issue from a code-review or runtime finding, with backlink to the source PR/comment and the project's required fields populated. Use when a defect or non-blocking polish item is identified during /review or normal work, and working-agreement #3 ("defects → GitHub issue, never silent fixes") applies.
4	disable-model-invocation: true
5	---
6	
7	# gh-issue-from-finding
8	
9	## Purpose
10	
11	Operationalize working-agreement #3: every defect (and every deferred-but-known polish item) becomes a GitHub issue *before* it gets fixed. This skill compresses the manual ceremony of writing a well-formatted issue into one command.
12	
13	## Usage
14	
15	User invokes with a short description of the finding. Required context the skill must collect:
16	- `--from-pr <N>` — source PR where the finding was identified (optional but strongly preferred for traceability)
17	- `--from-comment <url>` — direct link to the /review comment (optional)
18	- `--severity critical|high|medium|low` (defaults to `medium`; use `critical` only for production-down / data-loss; never use `critical` for security — see `--security` flag below)
19	- `--type bug|enhancement|documentation` (defaults to `bug`; maps to GitHub label)
20	- `--security` — flag for security-adjacent issues. If set, the skill SHOULD NOT proceed with `gh issue create`; instead it prints the GitHub Security Advisories URL and exits, per the security-disclosure policy (see issue [#9](https://github.com/TheurgicDuke771/DataQ/issues/9) for context).
21	- `--blocker-for-week <N>` — optional, marks the issue as a Week-N blocker.
22	
23	## Steps
24	
25	1. **If `--security` is set**, do not create a public issue. Print:
26	   ```
27	   Security-adjacent finding — do not file as a public issue.
28	   Report via GitHub Security Advisory:
29	   https://github.com/TheurgicDuke771/DataQ/security/advisories/new
30	   ```
31	   Exit successfully without calling `gh issue create`.
32	
33	2. **Resolve the label** from `--type`:
34	   - `bug` → `bug`
35	   - `enhancement` → `enhancement`
36	   - `documentation` → `documentation`
37	
38	3. **Build the issue title** as a plain descriptive sentence (not a `fix:` / `feat:` prefix — that belongs on the fixing PR). Examples:
39	   - "ADR template missing Consulted field"
40	   - "Architecture diagram doesn't include Apache Airflow"
41	
42	4. **Build the issue body** using the template below.
43	
44	5. **Call `gh issue create`.** The body template contains backticks, code blocks, and `$`-signs that break shell-interpolated `--body "..."` quoting. Use `--body-file` with a temp file instead:
45	   ```bash
46	   tmp=$(mktemp); printf '%s' "$BODY" > "$tmp"
47	   gh issue create --title "$TITLE" --label "$LABEL" --body-file "$tmp"
48	   rm "$tmp"
49	   ```
50	   Capture the returned URL.
51	
52	6. **Print to the user:**
53	   ```
54	   Filed: <issue url>
55	   Backlink it in the source PR with:
56	     gh pr comment <N> --body "Tracked as <issue url>"
57	   When the fix PR lands, include `Fixes #<issue number>` in its body.
58	   ```
59	
60	## Body template
61	
62	```markdown
63	<one-line problem statement>
64	
65	## Items
66	
67	- [ ] <actionable item 1>
68	- [ ] <actionable item 2>
69	
70	## Why this is a follow-up, not a same-PR fix
71	<one sentence — typically "non-load-bearing for current PR" or "must land before Week N">
72	
73	## Source
74	`/review` comment: <comment URL if provided>
75	Source PR: #<N> (if provided)
76	```
77	
78	If `--blocker-for-week N` is set, prepend a bold line to the body:
79	
80	> **Must land before Week N (<short reason>).**
81	
82	## Rules
83	
84	- **Never create a public issue when `--security` is set.** Always route to Security Advisories.
85	- **Issue title is a plain descriptive sentence.** No `fix:` / `feat:` prefix on issues — those are PR-side conventional types per ADR 0002. Issue templates at `.github/ISSUE_TEMPLATE/bug.md` currently pre-fill the wrong prefix; this skill counteracts that until issue [#8](https://github.com/TheurgicDuke771/DataQ/issues/8) is resolved.
86	- **Backlink is the source of truth.** Always include the /review comment URL or source PR number so the issue can be cross-referenced.
87	- **Fix PR must reference `Fixes #N`** to auto-close on merge (working-agreement #3 + PR template's "Linked issue" section).
88	- **Do not create the fix branch from this skill.** Branch creation is a separate workflow.
89	
90	## Anti-patterns to avoid
91	
92	- Don't create an issue for findings on an *unmerged* PR — fix those in-place with a fixup commit on the branch. Issues are for findings deferred to a later PR or found after merge (see [/review-before-merge memory rule](https://github.com/TheurgicDuke771/DataQ/blob/main/CLAUDE.md)).
93	- Don't bulk-create issues for trivial nits. Use this for items that genuinely need tracking — typos and one-line clarity fixes can be done inline.
94	- Don't include sensitive details (customer names, secrets, internal URLs, exploit payloads) in a public issue. If those are involved, the finding belongs in a Security Advisory.
95	
```

1	---
2	name: migration-safety
3	description: Specialized reviewer for Alembic migrations under backend/alembic/versions/. Audits for backward-incompatible operations that would break running code during a deploy, per working-agreement #24 ("backward-compatible migrations only — no DROP COLUMN + code change in the same PR"). Use proactively on every PR that adds, modifies, or removes a file under backend/alembic/versions/. Also invoke when the user asks "is this migration safe?" or before any production deploy from Week 5 onward.
4	tools: Read, Grep, Glob, Bash
5	model: sonnet
6	---
7	
8	You are a specialized Alembic migration reviewer enforcing **backward-compatible migrations only** per [working-agreement #24](../../CLAUDE.md).
9	
10	## Why this matters
11	
12	From Week 5 onward, `results` and `pipeline_runs` will hold real data. A migration that drops a column or renames it in the same release as the code change crashes any worker still running the old image during rollout. Two-step migrations (deploy code that tolerates both shapes → migrate → deploy code that assumes new shape) are mandatory.
13	
14	## What you check
15	
16	Audit every `.py` file under `backend/alembic/versions/` that's added or modified in the diff. Use `gh pr diff <N>` if a PR number is provided, otherwise `git diff main...HEAD -- 'backend/alembic/versions/*.py'`.
17	
18	### 🔴 Hard violations (block merge)
19	
20	Each of these is unsafe under concurrent rolling deploy and must be split into a two-step migration:
21	
22	1. **`op.drop_column(...)`** — dropping a column the old code may still reference. Two-step: (1) deploy code that stops reading/writing the column; (2) later release drops the column.
23	2. **`op.drop_table(...)`** — same reasoning at table level.
24	3. **`op.alter_column(..., nullable=False)`** where the column was previously nullable and the migration does **not** include a `server_default` to backfill. Old code writing NULL will fail.
25	4. **`op.alter_column(..., type_=...)`** changing a column type in a non-implicit way (e.g., `String → Integer`, `Integer → UUID`). Implicit widening (`String(50) → String(255)`) is acceptable; type *changes* are not.
26	5. **`op.rename_column(...)`** — same operational issue as drop+add. Must be a two-step: (1) add new column, dual-write in code; (2) backfill; (3) flip reads; (4) later release drops the old column.
27	6. **`op.rename_table(...)`** — same reasoning.
28	7. **`op.drop_constraint(...)` for FK or unique** where the constraint enforces data invariants the application depends on. Acceptable only if explicitly noted as part of a planned rollout.
29	8. **Missing `downgrade()` body** (only `pass`) for any non-trivial migration. Rollback path must exist per the migration PR checklist in `.github/pull_request_template.md`.
30	9. **DDL inside a single transaction with DML on the same table** — risks deadlocks on production-sized tables. Heuristic: presence of `op.execute("UPDATE ...")` followed by structural changes in the same migration.
31	10. **`op.execute("...")` containing destructive SQL keywords** — raw SQL bypasses the `op.*` pattern matchers above. Scan the string argument for `DROP`, `RENAME`, `ALTER ... TYPE`, `ALTER ... SET NOT NULL` (without `DEFAULT`), `TRUNCATE`. Same backward-compatibility rules apply as for the equivalent `op.*` calls.
32	
33	### 🟡 Yellow flags (call out, don't necessarily block)
34	
35	1. **Index creation without `CONCURRENTLY`** on Postgres for a table that will be large (`runs`, `results`, `pipeline_runs`). Use `op.create_index(..., postgresql_concurrently=True)` and a separate transaction.
36	2. **No data backfill plan** for a new NOT NULL column with no `server_default`.
37	3. **Migration touches tables in two unrelated domains** in one revision (e.g., `connections` and `pipeline_runs` together). Suggests two migrations bundled.
38	4. **No docstring at the top of the migration file** describing intent and the rollout sequence.
39	5. **`down_revision` doesn't match the previously-latest migration** — possible merge conflict that got resolved incorrectly.
40	
41	### 🟢 Acceptable patterns
42	
43	- Add nullable column → backfill in a separate release → set NOT NULL → drop default. Three migrations, three releases.
44	- Adding a new table.
45	- Adding a new index using `postgresql_concurrently=True` in its own migration.
46	- Adding constraints in `NOT VALID` mode, then `VALIDATE CONSTRAINT` in a later migration.
47	
48	## How to report
49	
50	Produce a structured report:
51	
52	1. **🔴 Hard violations** — file:line, operation, why it's unsafe, suggested two-step split.
53	2. **🟡 Concerns** — file:line, operation, suggested change.
54	3. **Rollback check** — for each migration, confirm `downgrade()` is implemented and inverts `upgrade()`.
55	4. **Two-step plan** (if a violation requires it) — outline the staged rollout the user should adopt.
56	5. **✅ Verdict** — one of:
57	   - `Pass — migration is backward-compatible.`
58	   - `Conditional — N concerns. Discuss with whoever runs the deploy.`
59	   - `Block — N hard violations. Must split into two-step rollout before merge.`
60	
61	## Operational note
62	
63	This agent reads migration files and reports — it does NOT modify them. The author splits the migration based on the report. Two-step migrations land as two separate PRs (one per step) per working-agreement #1.
64	
65	## Source documents (your authority)
66	
67	- [CLAUDE.md §6 Database — backward-compatible migrations only](../../CLAUDE.md)
68	- [.github/pull_request_template.md — Schema migration checklist](../../.github/pull_request_template.md)
69	
```

{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/arijit/Coding/Python/DataQ/.claude/agents/migration-safety.md",
  "old_string": "You are a specialized Alembic migration reviewer enforcing **backward-compatible migrations only** per [working-agreement #24](../../CLAUDE.md).",

{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/arijit/Coding/Python/DataQ/.claude/agents/migration-safety.md",
  "old_string": "### 🟢 Acceptable patterns\n\n- Add nullable column → backfill in a separate release → set NOT NULL → drop default. Three migrations, three releases.\n- Adding a new table.\n- Adding a new index using `postgresql_concurrently=True` in its own migration.\n- Adding constraints in `NOT VALID` mode, then `VALIDATE CONSTRAINT` in a later migration.",
  "new_string": "### 🟢 Acceptable patterns\n\n- Add nullable column → backfill in a separate release → set NOT NULL → drop default. Three migrations, three releases.\n- Adding a new table.\n- Adding a new index using `postgresql_concurrently=True` in its own migration.\n- Adding constraints in `NOT VALID` mode, then `VALIDATE CONSTRAINT` in a later migration.\n\n### False positives to avoid\n\nDon't flag these — they look like violations but aren't:\n\n- **`drop_*` / destructive SQL inside the `downgrade()` body.** A `downgrade()` that drops the column/table the `upgrade()` added is the *correct* inverse, not a forward-migration violation. Only audit `upgrade()` for backward-compatibility.\n- **`drop_column` / `drop_table` in a brand-new revision that also created that same object in `upgrade()`.** A self-contained add-then-drop within one `upgrade()` (rare, e.g. a scratch temp table) touches nothing the old code knew about.\n- **Destructive keywords appearing in string literals, comments, or docstrings** (e.g. a docstring that says \"this does not DROP the column\"). Match actual `op.execute(\"...\")` SQL arguments, not prose.\n- **Type \"changes\" that are implicit widenings** (`String(50) → String(255)`, `Integer → BigInteger`) — these are safe; only flag narrowing or cross-family changes.\n- **`alter_column(nullable=False)` that *does* ship a `server_default`** — the backfill is present, so it's safe."
}
```

```
1	---
2	name: orchestration-abstraction-guard
3	description: Specialized reviewer that audits backend code for provider-specific branching that bypasses the OrchestrationProvider abstraction. Use this proactively when reviewing any PR that touches backend/app/orchestration/, backend/app/services/, or backend/app/api/ — especially any code path handling ADF or Airflow events, trigger bindings, or pipeline_runs. Also invoke when the user asks "does this go through the abstraction?" or similar.
4	tools: Read, Grep, Glob, Bash
5	model: sonnet
6	---
7	
8	You are a specialized code reviewer guarding the `OrchestrationProvider` abstraction defined in [ADR 0004](../../docs/adr/0004-orchestration-abstraction.md).
9	
10	## Your single concern
11	
12	ADR 0004's biggest stated risk is **provider-specific branching that bypasses the abstraction**. CLAUDE.md §11 lists it as the second item in "What NOT to do":
13	
14	> Don't bypass the `OrchestrationProvider` abstraction with provider-specific branching in service code.
15	
16	You exist to catch that exact regression. Nothing else.
17	
18	## What you check
19	
20	Audit the changed code (use `gh pr diff <N>` if a PR number is provided, otherwise `git diff main...HEAD`) for:
21	
22	### 🔴 Hard violations (must block merge)
23	
24	1. **Any switch on provider identity** outside the orchestration package. Includes ALL of the following forms — be exhaustive, not just `==`:
25	   ```python
26	   if provider == "adf": ...           # FORBIDDEN
27	   if provider != "airflow": ...        # FORBIDDEN
28	   if provider in ("adf", "airflow"):   # FORBIDDEN
29	   match provider:                       # FORBIDDEN
30	       case "adf": ...
31	   ```
32	   These belong inside `backend/app/orchestration/<provider>.py` modules only. Service-layer code should call methods on a resolved `OrchestrationProvider` instance, not switch on its identity.
33	
34	2. **Importing provider implementations directly into service or API code:**
35	   ```python
36	   from backend.app.orchestration.adf import AdfProvider   # FORBIDDEN in services/, api/
37	   ```
38	   Service code resolves providers through a registry / factory, not by importing concrete classes. **Test code is exempt** — see Acceptable patterns.
39	
40	3. **Webhook routes that bypass the agreed shape.** ADR 0004 defines explicit sibling routes `POST /api/v1/orchestration/events/adf` and `.../airflow` (the trailing path segment names the provider; it is not a `{provider}` capture). A route like `/api/v1/adf/events` — provider name outside the `/orchestration/events/` namespace — is the violation. The implemented `/api/v1/orchestration/events/adf` that resolves the provider via `get_orchestration_provider("adf")` is correct.
41	
42	4. **`pipeline_runs` queries with a hardcoded provider literal baked into shared logic:**
43	   ```python
44	   session.query(PipelineRun).filter(PipelineRun.provider == "adf")  # FORBIDDEN in provider-agnostic code
45	   # Acceptable: filter by a provider value passed in / resolved at the boundary:
46	   session.query(PipelineRun).filter(PipelineRun.provider == provider)
47	   ```
48	   Provider values are plain strings validated against `ORCHESTRATION_PROVIDERS` (`backend/app/db/models.py` — TEXT + CHECK, deliberately **not** a Python/PG enum). The smell is a literal `"adf"`/`"airflow"` hardcoded into provider-agnostic code, not the use of `str` itself.
49	
50	### 🟡 Yellow flags (call out, don't necessarily block)
51	
52	1. **A literal provider string** (`"adf"`, `"airflow"`) appearing in `backend/app/services/` or `backend/app/api/` outside the registry-resolution boundary, rather than the value flowing from `ORCHESTRATION_PROVIDERS` / a resolved provider. (Provider is a `str` by design — flag the hardcoded literal, not the type.)
53	2. **`TODO` / `FIXME` / `XXX` comments mentioning ADF or Airflow** — may indicate deferred work that should be tracked as an issue per working-agreement #3.
54	3. **Test data fixtures that only cover ADF**. Both providers should be exercised; ADF-only coverage suggests the abstraction has rotted.
55	4. **`trigger_bindings` rows hardcoded to a single provider** in seed data or fixtures — should at least demonstrate both providers.
56	
57	### 🟢 Acceptable patterns
58	
59	- Provider-specific code **inside** the orchestration package — `backend/app/orchestration/adf.py`, `airflow.py`, `base.py`, `registry.py` (file-per-provider; that's where it belongs).
60	- Routing by provider value through the registry at the boundary: `get_orchestration_provider(provider).parse_event(payload, headers)`.
61	- Provider-specific tests under `backend/tests/orchestration/` (e.g. `test_adf.py`, `test_adf_provider.py`).
62	- **Test code may import concrete provider implementations** (`AdfProvider`, `AirflowProvider`) for isolation testing — the abstraction rule binds production code (`backend/app/services/`, `backend/app/api/`), not tests.
63	
64	## How to report
65	
66	Produce a structured report with three sections:
67	
68	1. **🔴 Hard violations** (block-merge findings with file:line and the offending snippet)
69	2. **🟡 Concerns** (worth discussing, not necessarily blocking)
70	3. **✅ Verdict** — one of:
71	   - `Pass — no abstraction violations found.`
72	   - `Conditional — N concerns, no hard violations. Discuss before merge.`
73	   - `Block — N hard violations. Must fix before merge.`
74	
75	Be specific. Each finding must cite a file path and line range. Never speculate; only flag what's actually in the diff.
76	
77	## Source documents (your authority)
78	
79	- [ADR 0004 — Orchestration abstraction](../../docs/adr/0004-orchestration-abstraction.md)
80	- [CLAUDE.md §11 — What NOT to do](../../CLAUDE.md)
81	
```

  "new_string": "- **Test code may import concrete provider implementations** (`AdfProvider`, `AirflowProvider`) for isolation testing — the abstraction rule binds production code (`backend/app/services/`, `backend/app/api/`), not tests.\n\n## False positives to avoid\n\nDon't flag these — a provider name appearing in source is not automatically a branching violation:\n\n- **String literals inside docstrings or comments** — e.g. a comment `# ADF and Airflow both POST here` or a docstring listing supported providers. These are documentation, not branching.\n- **`@pytest.mark.parametrize` fixtures that enumerate providers** — e.g. `@pytest.mark.parametrize(\"provider\", [\"adf\", \"airflow\"])`. Enumerating both providers in a test is *good* coverage (the opposite of the ADF-only smell), not a hardcoded-literal violation.\n- **Logging / metrics / structlog tags** — e.g. `log.info(\"event\", provider=provider)` or `metrics.tag(\"provider\", provider_str)`. Passing the provider value as an observability dimension is not switching on it.\n- **The provider name in a route's trailing path segment** — `/api/v1/orchestration/events/adf` is the agreed shape (the segment names the provider; the route resolves it via the registry). Only a provider name *outside* the `/orchestration/events/` namespace is the violation.\n- **`provider == provider` / value flowing from `ORCHESTRATION_PROVIDERS`** — comparing against a passed-in/resolved provider value is fine; only a hardcoded `\"adf\"`/`\"airflow\"` *literal* in provider-agnostic code is the smell.\n\n## How to report"
}

```
# Results filters: env/datasource hide runs when connection metadata is unavailable; pipeline tab swallows runs-load error

Follow-ups from the /code-review of PR #347 (Week 6 Phase 3 — results filters + orchestration polish).

### 1. Env / Datasource filters silently drop runs with unknown connection metadata
[Results.tsx](frontend/src/pages/Results.tsx) RunsTab joins each run's env/datasource via its suite's connection (`suiteMeta`). When the connection is **not in the caller's `listConnections` result** — e.g. a viewer of a *shared suite* who has no access to the underlying connection — `meta.env`/`meta.category` is `null`, so any non-'all' Environment/Datasource filter excludes those runs. The runs are legitimately visible under 'All' (listRuns is suite-scoped), so filtering hides the user's own visible runs.

There is also a sub-second transient: while `connectionsState` is still loading (or if it errors), the same null-metadata path empties the table under an active env/datasource filter, showing "No runs match these filters." with no error.

**Decision needed (product):** should a run with unknown datasource match a datasource filter, render as 'unknown', or be excluded? At minimum, disable the Env/Datasource selects until connections load, and surface a connections load error.

### 2. Pipeline-runs tab swallows the runs-load error
PipelineRunsTab fetches `listRuns` to correlate triggered DQ runs. If that fetch fails (the pipeline-runs fetch succeeding), `runsState.status === 'error'` is never checked → the **DQ run** column shows '—' for every row, a confidently-wrong 'no triggered runs' with no indication the join data failed.

Both are degraded-UX, not crashes; surfaced at high-effort review and deferred from PR #347.
```

> AGENT

I now understand both bugs. Part 1 (line 150-151): when `meta` is null — connection still loading, or a shared-suite viewer who can't see the underlying connection — `meta?.env` is `undefined`, which fails the `!== env` check and silently drops runs that are legitimately visible under "All". Part 2 (PipelineRunsTab): `runsState`'s error is never checked, so a failed join renders a confidently-wrong "no triggered runs."

The issue explicitly flags a product decision for part 1 that changes the implementation. Let me get your call on it.

> TOOL

I have a complete picture. The codebase uses the raw `<Alert>` directly everywhere (no shared banner component), so there's no reuse violation there. There is one precedent for `type="warning"` (LiveRunProgress) for a partial/degraded state, which the diff matches well. Let me note the `message`/`title` inconsistency exists project-wide (pre-existing, not introduced here).

Let me check the ADR/CLAUDE notes referenced (#348) and whether the derived consts are genuinely redundant.

The findings are clear from the code. Here is my review.

Inlining `connectionsState.status === 'error'` into the banner guard and dropping `connectionsReady` in favour of `disabled={connectionsState.status !== 'ok'}` removes two named bindings without losing readability.",
    "failure_scenario": "Three overlapping spellings of the same async-state check (`connectionsReady`, `connectionsFailed`, and the inline `=== 'loading'`) drift apart when someone later adds a fourth state to AsyncState (e.g. an 'empty' or refetch marker): a maintainer updating one predicate must remember to update the others, and the Select can end up enabled while the banner says the data is unavailable."
  },

Same single-use-alias pattern as the RunsTab consts; inline it for consistency with the rest of the file, which inlines `state.status === 'error'` directly at the early-return guards (lines 141, 320).",
    "failure_scenario": "Minor but it sets a per-tab convention (named const in one tab, inline in the early returns of the same component) that the next contributor has to reconcile; the value is computed after the early-return block that already branches on a different status, so a reader must scroll to confirm it isn't stale relative to a poll-driven re-render."

The new code is on the correct side; no change needed, flagging only so the reviewer doesn't 'fix' it the wrong way.",
    "failure_scenario": "No new debt introduced. If a maintainer pattern-matches against the `title=` siblings and switches these banners to `title=`, the heading text would vanish at runtime (antd Alert has no `title` prop), leaving an icon-only warning with no message."

Summary: No reuse violation — the codebase has no shared error/warning-banner component; every page renders `<Alert>` inline, so matching that pattern is correct, and `type="warning"` for a degraded-but-usable state already has precedent in `LiveRunProgress.tsx`. The keep-visible predicate and the `disabled`/`loading` Select gating are sound. The only actionable items are the three single-use derived consts (`connectionsReady`/`connectionsFailed`/`runsJoinFailed`) duplicating async-state checks that are also done inline — minor simplification/consistency, not correctness. The pre-existing `message` vs `title` Alert inconsistency is repo-wide and the new code is on the correct (`message`) side.

```
Now I understand the data model fully. Let me analyze the predicate edge cases.

`DATASOURCE_CATEGORY[conn.type]` can be `null` for orchestration types. But a suite's connection wouldn't be orchestration. Still, `meta.category` can be `null` legitimately (orchestration conn). The keep-visible semantics handle that.

Now the critical analysis of the keep-visible predicate interaction. Consider: connections loaded `ok`, but a particular suite's `connection_id` isn't in `connById` (e.g., suite references a connection the user can list suites for but the connection was deleted, or listConnections is suite-scoped differently). Then `meta.env = null`, `meta.category = null`. With keep-visible, that run shows under ANY env/datasource filter. That's the intended fix.

But here's a subtle correctness concern: when connections loaded fine and ALL connections are accessible, the keep-visible `!= null` guard is harmless. The fix is sound.

Let me think harder about one real issue: the disabled selects combined with the warning. When `connectionsFailed` (error), selects are disabled at `'all'`. Fine. But what about partial: suites failed to load but connections ok? Then `suiteMeta` is empty (returns early when `suitesState.status !== 'ok'`). So all `meta` are `undefined` → keep-visible → all runs shown regardless of env/category filter, even though selects are ENABLED (connectionsReady=ok). User picks env=QA, sees all runs unfiltered, with no warning. Let me check whether suites failing is surfaced anywhere.

The component only early-returns on `state` (runs). suitesState error is never surfaced. The suite filter would show only "All suites" (empty suiteOptions). That's a pre-existing condition not introduced by this diff, but the env/datasource filter being enabled-yet-inert when suites failed is an interaction worth noting. However, that's not newly introduced — pre-diff, env filter would have excluded everything (meta undefined → `meta?.env !== env` true → excluded all). So actually pre-diff: suites-fail + env-filter = empty table. Post-diff: suites-fail + env-filter = all runs shown. This is a behavior change but arguably the intended direction. Not a clear bug.

Let me verify there are no unused imports or removed guards. The diff didn't remove imports. Let me double check `Alert` was already imported (yes, line 2).

Let me check one more real concern: the `loading` prop on Select alongside `disabled`. That's purely cosmetic. Fine.

Let me confirm there's no issue with the `connectionsState` being used in `suiteMeta` memo when status is `loading` — it returns `[]` conns, so env/category null during load. During load, selects disabled, so user can't filter. Consistent.

I've found the analysis is largely clean. Let me reconsider the strand scenario once more and the suites-error interaction as the most concrete findings.

The most concrete real finding: **suitesState error is silently swallowed in RunsTab**, and post-diff the env/datasource filters become silently inert (enabled but ignored) in that case. But is `suitesState` error actually possible while runs load? Yes — independent fetches. Let me verify this is a genuine pre-existing vs new concern. Pre-diff, env filter excluded all rows (showed empty); the diff flips it to show-all. Neither is "correct" but the diff doesn't make it worse in a user-harmful way (showing data > hiding data). I'll note it as lower-confidence.

Let me also verify: is there any falsy-zero issue? `env`/`category` are string unions, never 0. No.

Based on thorough review, here are my findings.

```json
[
  {
    "file": "frontend/src/pages/Results.tsx",
    "line": 154,
    "summary": "When suitesState fails to load (independent fetch from runs), suiteMeta is empty so every run's meta is undefined; the new keep-visible predicate then shows ALL runs regardless of the Env/Datasource filter, while those selects remain ENABLED (gated only on connectionsState, not suitesState) with no warning. The user sets env=QA, the table ignores it silently, and no Alert explains why.",
    "failure_scenario": "listSuites 500s but listRuns and listConnections succeed. User picks Environment=QA expecting a subset; the table shows every run unfiltered with no error/warning, and the still-enabled selects make the filter appear functional. (Pre-diff this case wrongly hid all rows instead; the diff trades one silent-wrong behavior for another rather than surfacing the suites-load failure.)"
  },
  {
    "file": "frontend/src/pages/Results.tsx",
    "line": 190,
    "summary": "connectionsReady gates the Env/Datasource selects only on connectionsState==='ok', but the env/category facts also require suitesState==='ok' (suiteMeta returns an empty map unless suites loaded). So the selects can be enabled (connections ok) while the join cannot actually be computed (suites still loading or errored), letting the user choose a filter value that has no effect.",
    "failure_scenario": "connectionsState resolves 'ok' before suitesState resolves; for the window where suites is still 'loading' the Env/Datasource selects are interactive but suiteMeta is empty, so any non-'all' selection silently no-ops (all runs shown). The 'connectionsReady' name implies the join is ready when it is not."
  }
]
```

Summary: I read the full `Results.tsx` and the `useAsyncData` hook. The `AsyncState` shape (`'loading' | 'ok' | 'error'`) matches every assumption in the diff, so the `'ok'`/`'loading'`/`'error'` checks are correct. The core `meta?.env != null && meta.env !== env` keep-visible predicate is logically sound for the intended fix (unknown metadata stays visible; known-and-differing is excluded), with no falsy-zero or null-deref issues (env/category are string unions). The "strand the filter" concern does not materialize in `RunsTab` because there is no reload/poll there, so an `'ok'` connections state never reverts to disabled after the user sets a value. The PipelineRunsTab warning change is correct.

The two findings above are real but lower-severity interaction bugs: both stem from gating the Env/Datasource controls and the keep-visible logic on `connectionsState` alone while the underlying join also depends on `suitesState`, which is never surfaced and not part of the gate. The worse of the two is the silent suites-load failure, where the diff changes behavior from "hide everything" to "show everything while the filter appears active."

# Results drill-down: expose redacted sample failing rows

## Context
PR-C0b (#225) shipped the run/result read API. `ResultRead` (`backend/app/api/v1/runs.py`) **deliberately omits `results.sample_failures`** (the raw GX failing rows). They can carry real data and are treated as redactor-only elsewhere (`gx_runner` notes the sample keys reach logs only via the redactor; the dry-run path omits them for the same reason; CLAUDE.md mandates PII redaction). `sanitize_json` only fixes non-finite floats — it is **not** PII redaction.

## Ask
The Results page (PR-C1) drill-down wants sample failing rows. Expose them **safely**:
- Add a **row-level redaction** / opt-in policy at the API boundary before returning `sample_failures` (same redaction/opt-in seam ADR 0011 / progress.md flags for `ResultPublisher`, since the rows leave DataQ's trust boundary).
- Then add `sample_failures` (redacted) back to `ResultRead` and gate appropriately.

Suite-level `view` authz is already enforced; this is specifically about not shipping unredacted PII to view-sharers.

## Acceptance
- Redacted sample rows available on `GET /runs/{id}`; test proves raw values are masked.
- Land with or before PR-C1.

_Surfaced by the PR-C0b multi-agent review._
```

22	
23	from backend.app.datasources.base import CheckOutcome, CheckSpec, SuiteOutcome
24	
25	# GX result keys that describe failing rows — copied into CheckOutcome.sample_failures.
26	# These may contain real data, so they only ever reach logs via the redactor.
27	_SAMPLE_KEYS = ("partial_unexpected_list", "unexpected_count", "unexpected_percent")
28	
29	# GX injects internal bookkeeping keys into expectation_config.kwargs at run time
30	# (e.g. batch_id); strip them so expected_value persists only the check's own
31	# parameters.
32	_GX_INTERNAL_KWARGS = frozenset({"batch_id"})
33	
34	
35	class UnknownExpectationError(ValueError):
36	    """Raised when a check's expectation_type has no matching GX expectation."""
37	
38	
39	def _expectation_class_name(expectation_type: str) -> str:
40	    """snake_case GX type → PascalCase class name.
41	
42	    ``expect_column_values_to_not_be_null`` → ``ExpectColumnValuesToNotBeNull``.
43	    """
44	    return "".join(part.title() for part in expectation_type.split("_"))
45	
46	
47	def _to_gx_expectation(spec: CheckSpec) -> Any:
48	    class_name = _expectation_class_name(spec.expectation_type)
49	    expectation_cls = getattr(gxe, class_name, None)
50	    if expectation_cls is None:
51	        raise UnknownExpectationError(
52	            f"Unknown expectation_type {spec.expectation_type!r} (no gx class {class_name!r})"
53	        )
54	    return expectation_cls(**spec.kwargs)
55	
56	
57	def _extract_sample_failures(result: dict[str, Any]) -> dict[str, Any] | None:
58	    sample = {key: result[key] for key in _SAMPLE_KEYS if key in result}
59	    return sample or None
60	
61	
62	def _check_errored(exception_info: Any) -> tuple[bool, str | None]:
63	    """Did this expectation raise while being evaluated? (GX `exception_info`).
64	
65	    GX (1.17) reports `exception_info` in two shapes per `ExpectationValidationResult`:
66	
67	    * a **flat** dict ``{'raised_exception': bool, 'exception_message': str|None,
68	      ...}`` for a cleanly-evaluated expectation, and
69	    * a dict **keyed by `MetricConfigurationID`** when a metric computation raised,
70	      each value being a flat dict with its own ``raised_exception``.
71	
72	    Treat the check as errored if the flat form raised, or any keyed entry did;
73	    return the first exception message for debuggability. An errored check is an
74	    ``error`` result (#122), not a data ``fail``.
75	    """
76	    if not isinstance(exception_info, dict) or not exception_info:
77	        return False, None
78	    if "raised_exception" in exception_info:  # flat shape
79	        return bool(exception_info.get("raised_exception")), exception_info.get("exception_message")
80	    # keyed-by-metric shape: errored if any metric computation raised
81	    for entry in exception_info.values():
```

47	def _redact_pii(_logger: Any, _name: str, event_dict: EventDict) -> EventDict:

2

4	results page. The DQ-run reads (`/runs`, `/runs/{id}`) are **suite-scoped**: the
5	list filters to suites the caller can access and the detail gates on
6	`require_permission(view)`, so per-suite sharing + existence-hiding hold exactly
7	as for suites/checks (this is why reading Postgres directly — e.g. a Grafana
8	panel — is rejected as the primary surface: it bypasses this authz + the sample
9	redaction below). `/pipeline_runs` is orchestration monitoring, not suite-scoped,
10	so it is gated on authentication only.

139	@router.get("/runs/{run_id}", response_model=RunDetailRead, summary="Get a run with its results")
140	def get_run(
141	    run_id: uuid.UUID,
142	    current_user: Annotated[User, Depends(get_current_user)],
143	    db: Annotated[Session, Depends(get_db)],
144	) -> RunDetailRead:
145	    run = svc.get_run(db, run_id)
146	    if run is None:
147	        raise HTTPException(status_code=status.HTTP_404_NOT_FOUND, detail="run not found")
148	    # Gate on the run's suite: a caller who can't see the suite can't see its
149	    # runs (404 hides the run id too, matching the suite existence-hiding rule).
150	    require_permission(db, run.suite_id, current_user.id, minimum="view")
151	    results = svc.list_results(db, run_id)
152	    # `Run` has no `results` relationship to validate a RunDetailRead from
153	    # directly, so validate the run fields (as RunRead) and graft the
154	    # separately-fetched, redaction-gated results on.
155	    return RunDetailRead(
156	        **RunRead.model_validate(run).model_dump(),
157	        results=[ResultRead.model_validate(r) for r in results],
158	    )

346	# ── retention sweep (configurable PII purge of old result samples) ────────────
347	
348	
349	def purge_expired_sample_failures(
350	    session: Session, *, retention_days: int, now: datetime | None = None
351	) -> int:
352	    """Scrub `sample_failures` from results older than ``retention_days``.
353	
354	    ``sample_failures`` is the only result column that can carry real (possibly
355	    PII-bearing) data rows; after the retention window we null it out (to a true
356	    SQL NULL) and stamp ``sample_failures_purged_at`` so the purge is auditable.
357	    The result row itself — and crucially ``metric_value`` — is **kept**, so
358	    dashboard trends / anomaly baselines survive the purge (ADR 0012); this is a
359	    PII-minimisation sweep, not a run-history delete. Returns the rows scrubbed.
360	
361	    Only rows that actually hold a sample *object* are touched: the JSONB column
362	    stores Python ``None`` as JSON ``'null'`` (``none_as_null`` defaults False),
363	    and passing/errored checks write that — so ``IS NOT NULL`` would over-match
364	    millions of empty rows. ``jsonb_typeof`` excludes both SQL NULL (→ NULL) and
365	    JSON ``'null'`` (→ ``'null'``), leaving only real ``object``/``array``
366	    samples. Naturally idempotent (a scrubbed row is SQL NULL → typeof NULL →
367	    excluded); the ``purged_at IS NULL`` guard makes that intent explicit.
368	
369	    ``retention_days <= 0`` disables the sweep (returns 0 without touching the DB)
370	    — a clean off-switch rather than purging everything. The cutoff is anchored on
371	    ``Result.created_at`` (when the result landed ≈ when the run completed).
372	    """
373	    if retention_days <= 0:
374	        return 0
375	    moment = now or _now()
376	    cutoff = moment - timedelta(days=retention_days)
377	    sample_typeof = func.jsonb_typeof(Result.sample_failures)
378	    # session.execute(<DML>) returns a CursorResult; the typed overload widens it
379	    # to Result (no rowcount), so cast to read the affected-row count.
380	    purge_result = cast(
381	        CursorResult[Any],
382	        session.execute(
383	            update(Result)
384	            .where(
385	                Result.created_at < cutoff,
386	                Result.sample_failures_purged_at.is_(None),
387	                sample_typeof.isnot(None),
388	                sample_typeof != "null",
389	            )
390	            .values(sample_failures=null(), sample_failures_purged_at=moment)
391	            # Fire-and-forget bulk DML on a fresh, short-lived worker session with
392	            # no loaded Result identities — skip the ORM identity-map sync, which
393	            # under the default 'auto'/'fetch' would emit an extra SELECT of every
394	            # matching PK before the UPDATE (the WHERE uses jsonb_typeof, so the
395	            # in-Python 'evaluate' strategy can't apply).
396	            .execution_options(synchronize_session=False)
397	        ),
398	    )
399	    session.commit()
400	    purged = purge_result.rowcount
401	    log.info(
402	        "sample_failures_purged",
403	        purged=purged,
404	        retention_days=retention_days,
```

```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/arijit/Coding/Python/DataQ/backend/app/services/run_service.py",
  "old_string": "# ── retention sweep (configurable PII purge of old result samples) ────────────",
  "new_string": "# ── sample-failures redaction (PII-safe surfacing on the read API) ────────────\n\n# Aggregate summary keys in a GX sample are counts/percentages, not row data, so\n# they are safe to surface. Everything else — notably `partial_unexpected_list`,\n# the raw offending cell values — is treated as potential PII and masked.\n_SAMPLE_SAFE_KEYS = frozenset({\"unexpected_count\", \"unexpected_percent\"})\n_REDACTED_VALUE = \"<redacted>\"\n\n\ndef _redact_sample_value(value: Any) -> Any:\n    \"\"\"Mask data values while preserving container shape and dict keys.\n\n    List length and row-dict column names are *schema*, not row data, so they\n    stay (they tell the viewer how many rows / which columns failed); every leaf\n    value is replaced with ``\"<redacted>\"``.\n    \"\"\"\n    if isinstance(value, dict):\n        return {key: _redact_sample_value(val) for key, val in value.items()}\n    if isinstance(value, list):\n        return [_redact_sample_value(item) for item in value]\n    return _REDACTED_VALUE\n\n\ndef redact_sample_failures(sample: dict[str, Any] | None) -> dict[str, Any] | None:\n    \"\"\"Redact a result's `sample_failures` for safe surfacing on the read API.\n\n    `sample_failures` carries aggregate counts plus `partial_unexpected_list` —\n    the actual failing cell values, which can be PII. Suite-level ``view`` authz\n    lets share-recipients read a suite's results, so the raw rows must not cross\n    that boundary unredacted (CLAUDE.md PII rule; the column is redactor-only\n    everywhere else and is purged on the retention sweep below).\n\n    Policy: keep the numeric summary keys; mask every value in the rest,\n    preserving shape (list length, row-dict column names) so the drill-down shows\n    *how much* failed without leaking *what*. ``None`` (passing / errored /\n    purged results) passes through unchanged. A future opt-in could surface raw\n    rows to privileged viewers — that policy seam lives at this one call site.\n    \"\"\"\n    if not sample:\n        return None\n    return {\n        key: (value if key in _SAMPLE_SAFE_KEYS else _redact_sample_value(value))\n        for key, value in sample.items()\n    }\n\n\n# ── retention sweep (configurable PII purge of old result samples) ────────────"
}
