1	import { ArrowLeftOutlined, DownloadOutlined } from '@ant-design/icons';
2	import { Alert, Button, Card, Dropdown, Empty, Flex, Spin, Table, Tag, Typography } from 'antd';
3	import type { ColumnsType } from 'antd/es/table';
4	import { useMemo } from 'react';
5	import { useNavigate, useParams } from 'react-router-dom';
6	
7	import { getRun, type Result, type ResultStatus } from '../api/runs';
8	import { type Check, getSuite, listChecks } from '../api/suites';
9	import { CheckTrend } from '../components/checks/CheckTrend';
10	import {
11	  formatDuration,
12	  formatTimestamp,
13	  RESULT_STATUS_COLORS,
14	  RUN_STATUS_COLORS,
15	} from '../components/results/resultsFormat';
16	import { Page } from '../components/layout/Page';
17	import { ScalarValue } from '../components/results/ScalarValue';
18	import { useAsyncData } from '../hooks/useAsyncData';
19	import { downloadCsv, downloadJson, toFilenameStem } from '../utils/download';
20	
21	/** The four severity tiers that count as "evaluated" (ADR 0005) — skip/error don't. */
22	const SEVERITY_STATUSES = new Set<ResultStatus>(['pass', 'warn', 'fail', 'critical']);
23	
24	/**
25	 * Routed run-detail page (`/results/:runId`, ADR 0022) — replaces the run-detail
26	 * drawer so a run is deep-linkable and refreshable. Loads the run + its results
27	 * by id, plus the suite name and per-check names for display.
28	 *
29	 * Sample failing rows stay withheld by the API (PII, #226 / ADR 0018) — this
30	 * page never requests or renders them.
31	 */
32	export function RunDetail() {
33	  const navigate = useNavigate();
34	  const { runId } = useParams<{ runId: string }>();
35	
36	  const { state } = useAsyncData(async () => {
37	    if (!runId) throw new Error('no run');
38	    const run = await getRun(runId);
39	    // The suite may be readable while details race; tolerate a missing name/checks
40	    // rather than failing the whole page.
41	    const [suite, checks] = await Promise.all([
42	      getSuite(run.suite_id).catch(() => null),
43	      listChecks(run.suite_id).catch(() => [] as Check[]),
44	    ]);
45	    return { run, suiteName: suite?.name ?? null, checks };
46	  });
47	
48	  const back = () => navigate('/results');
49	
50	  return (
51	    <Page width={1000} gap={16}>
52	      <div>
53	        <Button type="text" icon={<ArrowLeftOutlined />} onClick={back} style={{ paddingLeft: 0 }}>
54	          Results
55	        </Button>
56	      </div>
57	
58	      {state.status === 'loading' && <Spin tip="Loading run…" size="large" />}
59	      {state.status === 'error' && (
60	        <Alert type="error" showIcon message="Failed to load run" description={state.error} />
61	      )}
62	      {state.status === 'ok' && (
63	        <RunDetailBody
64	          run={state.data.run}
65	          suiteName={state.data.suiteName}
66	          checks={state.data.checks}
67	        />
68	      )}
69	    </Page>
70	  );
71	}
72	
73	function RunDetailBody({
74	  run,
75	  suiteName,
76	  checks,
77	}: {
78	  run: Awaited<ReturnType<typeof getRun>>;
79	  suiteName: string | null;
80	  checks: Check[];
81	}) {
82	  const checksById = useMemo(() => {
83	    const map = new Map<string, Check>();
84	    for (const c of checks) map.set(c.id, c);
85	    return map;
86	  }, [checks]);
87	
88	  // "Checks passed" counts only evaluated (severity-tier) results — skip/error
89	  // didn't evaluate a severity, so they're excluded from the denominator, same
90	  // as the ADR-0005 health score (a run with skipped checks shouldn't read worse
91	  // than its health).
92	  const evaluated = run.results.filter((r) => SEVERITY_STATUSES.has(r.status));
93	  const passed = evaluated.filter((r) => r.status === 'pass').length;
94	
95	  return (
96	    <Flex vertical gap={16}>
97	      <Flex justify="space-between" align="center" gap={12} wrap>
98	        <Typography.Title level={3} style={{ margin: 0 }}>
99	          {suiteName ?? `Run ${run.suite_id.slice(0, 8)}`}
100	        </Typography.Title>
101	        <DownloadMenu run={run} suiteName={suiteName} checks={checksById} />
102	      </Flex>
103	
104	      {/* Equal-width cards that fill the row so its right edge lines up with the
105	          results table below (auto-fit + 1fr stretches them to the full width). */}
106	      <div
107	        style={{
108	          display: 'grid',
109	          gridTemplateColumns: 'repeat(auto-fit, minmax(150px, 1fr))',
110	          gap: 12,
111	        }}
112	      >
113	        <Stat label="Status">
114	          <Tag color={RUN_STATUS_COLORS[run.status]}>{run.status}</Tag>
115	        </Stat>
116	        <Stat label="Checks passed">
117	          {evaluated.length === 0 ? '—' : `${passed} / ${evaluated.length}`}
118	        </Stat>
119	        <Stat label="Triggered by">{run.triggered_by ?? '—'}</Stat>
120	        <Stat label="Started">{formatTimestamp(run.started_at)}</Stat>
121	        <Stat label="Duration">{formatDuration(run.started_at, run.finished_at)}</Stat>
122	      </div>
123	
124	      <ResultsTable results={run.results} checks={checksById} suiteId={run.suite_id} />
125	    </Flex>
126	  );
127	}
128	
129	function Stat({ label, children }: { label: string; children: React.ReactNode }) {
130	  return (
131	    <Card size="small" style={{ height: '100%' }}>
132	      <Flex vertical gap={4}>
133	        <Typography.Text type="secondary" style={{ fontSize: 12 }}>
134	          {label}
135	        </Typography.Text>
136	        <span style={{ fontSize: 15 }}>{children}</span>
137	      </Flex>
138	    </Card>
139	  );
140	}
141	
142	// ─────────────────────────── export (CSV / JSON) ────────────────────
143	
144	type RunWithResults = Awaited<ReturnType<typeof getRun>>;
145	
146	/** Compact, stable string for a JSONB scalar in a flat export cell. */
147	function exportScalar(value: Record<string, unknown> | null): string {
148	  return value === null ? '' : JSON.stringify(value);
149	}
150	
151	function DownloadMenu({
152	  run,
153	  suiteName,
154	  checks,
155	}: {
156	  run: RunWithResults;
157	  suiteName: string | null;
158	  checks: Map<string, Check>;
159	}) {
160	  const stem = `${toFilenameStem(suiteName ?? 'run')}_run_${run.id.slice(0, 8)}`;
161	  const checkName = (id: string) => checks.get(id)?.name ?? id;
162	  const expectation = (id: string) => checks.get(id)?.expectation_type ?? '';
163	
164	  const exportCsv = () => {
165	    downloadCsv(
166	      `${stem}.csv`,
167	      ['check', 'expectation', 'status', 'metric_value', 'observed'],
168	      run.results.map((r) => [
169	        checkName(r.check_id),
170	        expectation(r.check_id),
171	        r.status,
172	        r.metric_value,
173	        exportScalar(r.observed_value),
174	      ]),
175	    );
176	  };
177	
178	  const exportJson = () => {
179	    // Sample failing rows are never in the payload (PII, withheld by the API).
180	    downloadJson(`${stem}.json`, {
181	      run: {
182	        id: run.id,
183	        suite_id: run.suite_id,
184	        suite_name: suiteName,
185	        status: run.status,
186	        triggered_by: run.triggered_by,
187	        started_at: run.started_at,
188	        finished_at: run.finished_at,
189	      },
190	      checks: run.results.map((r) => ({
191	        check: checkName(r.check_id),
192	        expectation_type: expectation(r.check_id) || null,
193	        status: r.status,
194	        metric_value: r.metric_value,
195	        observed_value: r.observed_value,
196	        expected_value: r.expected_value,
197	      })),
198	    });
199	  };
200	
201	  return (
202	    <Dropdown
203	      menu={{
204	        items: [
205	          { key: 'csv', label: 'Download CSV', onClick: exportCsv },
206	          { key: 'json', label: 'Download JSON', onClick: exportJson },
207	        ],
208	      }}
209	      disabled={run.results.length === 0}
210	    >
211	      <Button icon={<DownloadOutlined />}>Download</Button>
212	    </Dropdown>
213	  );
214	}
215	
216	function ResultsTable({
217	  results,
218	  checks,
219	  suiteId,
220	}: {
221	  results: Result[];
222	  checks: Map<string, Check>;
223	  suiteId: string;
224	}) {
225	  if (results.length === 0) {
226	    return <Empty description="No check results — the run did not complete." />;
227	  }
228	  const columns: ColumnsType<Result> = [
229	    {
230	      title: 'Check',
231	      dataIndex: 'check_id',
232	      render: (id: string) =>
233	        checks.get(id)?.name ?? <Typography.Text code>{id.slice(0, 8)}</Typography.Text>,
234	    },
235	    {
236	      title: 'Expectation',
237	      dataIndex: 'check_id',
238	      render: (id: string) => (
239	        <Typography.Text type="secondary" style={{ fontSize: 12 }}>
240	          {checks.get(id)?.expectation_type ?? '—'}
241	        </Typography.Text>
242	      ),
243	    },
244	    {
245	      title: 'Status',
246	      dataIndex: 'status',
247	      width: 100,
248	      render: (s: ResultStatus) => <Tag color={RESULT_STATUS_COLORS[s]}>{s}</Tag>,
249	    },
250	    {
251	      title: 'Metric',
252	      dataIndex: 'metric_value',
253	      width: 90,
254	      render: (v: number | null) => (v === null ? '—' : v),
255	    },
256	    {
257	      title: 'Observed',
258	      dataIndex: 'observed_value',
259	      render: (v: Record<string, unknown> | null) => <ScalarValue value={v} />,
260	    },
261	  ];
262	  return (
263	    <Table
264	      rowKey="id"
265	      size="small"
266	      columns={columns}
267	      dataSource={results}
268	      pagination={false}
269	      expandable={{
270	        // Lazily fetch a check's metric trend only when its row is expanded —
271	        // keyed by check_id so each row's chart fetches its own history.
272	        expandedRowRender: (record) => (
273	          <CheckTrend key={record.check_id} suiteId={suiteId} checkId={record.check_id} />
274	        ),
275	        rowExpandable: (record) => checks.has(record.check_id),
276	      }}
277	    />
278	  );
279	}
280	
```

```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/arijit/Coding/Python/DataQ/frontend/src/pages/RunDetail.tsx",
  "old_string": "function ResultsTable({\n  results,\n  checks,\n  suiteId,\n}: {\n  results: Result[];\n  checks: Map<string, Check>;\n  suiteId: string;\n}) {",
  "new_string": "/** Redacted failing-row sample for a check (#226). The API masks every cell\n *  value to \"<redacted>\"; we surface the counts and the row/column *shape* so a\n *  reviewer sees how much (and structurally what) failed without seeing PII. */\nfunction SampleFailures({ sample }: { sample: Record<string, unknown> | null }) {\n  if (!sample) return null;\n  const count = typeof sample.unexpected_count === 'number' ? sample.unexpected_count : null;\n  const percent = typeof sample.unexpected_percent === 'number' ? sample.unexpected_percent : null;\n  const rawList = sample.partial_unexpected_list;\n  // Entries are either row dicts ({col: value}) or bare scalars; normalise both\n  // to row objects so a single column-derived table renders them.\n  const rows: Record<string, unknown>[] = Array.isArray(rawList)\n    ? rawList.map((entry) =>\n        entry !== null && typeof entry === 'object'\n          ? (entry as Record<string, unknown>)\n          : { value: entry },\n      )\n    : [];\n  const colKeys = [...new Set(rows.flatMap((r) => Object.keys(r)))];\n  const columns: ColumnsType<Record<string, unknown>> = colKeys.map((key) => ({\n    title: key,\n    dataIndex: key,\n    render: (v: unknown) => (\n      <Typography.Text type=\"secondary\" style={{ fontSize: 12 }}>\n        {v === undefined ? '' : String(v)}\n      </Typography.Text>\n    ),\n  }));\n\n  return (\n    <Flex vertical gap={8}>\n      <Typography.Text strong style={{ fontSize: 13 }}>\n        Failing rows{' '}\n        <Typography.Text type=\"secondary\" style={{ fontWeight: 'normal' }}>\n          {count !== null && `· ${count} row${count === 1 ? '' : 's'}`}\n          {percent !== null && ` · ${percent}%`} · values redacted\n        </Typography.Text>\n      </Typography.Text>\n      {rows.length > 0 ? (\n        <Table<Record<string, unknown>>\n          rowKey={(_, i) => String(i)}\n          size=\"small\"\n          columns={columns}\n          dataSource={rows}\n          pagination={false}\n        />\n      ) : (\n        <Typography.Text type=\"secondary\" style={{ fontSize: 12 }}>\n          No sample rows captured.\n        </Typography.Text>\n      )}\n    </Flex>\n  );\n}\n\nfunction ResultsTable({\n  results,\n  checks,\n  suiteId,\n}: {\n  results: Result[];\n  checks: Map<string, Check>;\n  suiteId: string;\n}) {"
}

{
  "replace_all": false,

  "old_string": "      expandable={{\n        // Lazily fetch a check's metric trend only when its row is expanded —\n        // keyed by check_id so each row's chart fetches its own history.\n        expandedRowRender: (record) => (\n          <CheckTrend key={record.check_id} suiteId={suiteId} checkId={record.check_id} />\n        ),\n        rowExpandable: (record) => checks.has(record.check_id),\n      }}",
  "new_string": "      expandable={{\n        // Lazily fetch a check's metric trend only when its row is expanded —\n        // keyed by check_id so each row's chart fetches its own history. The\n        // redacted failing-row sample (if any) sits below the trend.\n        expandedRowRender: (record) => (\n          <Flex vertical gap={16}>\n            <CheckTrend key={record.check_id} suiteId={suiteId} checkId={record.check_id} />\n            <SampleFailures sample={record.sample_failures} />\n          </Flex>\n        ),\n        // Expandable when we can show a trend (known check) or a failing sample.\n        rowExpandable: (record) => checks.has(record.check_id) || record.sample_failures !== null,\n      }}"
}

I have enough to assess. Let me think about the frontend `String(v)` on object case carefully.

In `partial_unexpected_list`, after redaction by the backend, a row dict like `{id: "<redacted>", email: "<redacted>"}` — entries are objects, kept as-is. `colKeys` collects `id`, `email`. The render `String(v)` gets the redacted string. Fine.

But what about a nested structure? If a row entry were `{addr: {city: "<redacted>"}}` (nested dict, masked at leaves by backend), the column `addr` renders `String({city:"<redacted>"})` = `"[object Object]"`. Not a leak (value already redacted), just ugly display. Minor.

One real concern: `count === 1` pluralization at line 250 — uses `count !== null &&` short-circuit. If count is 0, `0 row${...}` → "0 rows" since `0 === 1` is false. Correct. If `count` is null but the line renders, `count !== null` is false → empty string. Fine. Pluralization correct.

Now let me think hard about whether `_SAMPLE_SAFE_KEYS` passthrough could leak. The values come from gx_runner's `_SAMPLE_KEYS` extraction — `unexpected_count` (int) and `unexpected_percent` (float). These are GX-internal aggregates, never row data. Not attacker/data-controlled (they're computed by GX, not column values). Safe.

But: `redact_sample_failures` passes these through **regardless of type**. If a future code path or a different GX expectation produced a non-scalar under those keys, it would leak verbatim. Currently safe but a latent fragility — the safe-key passthrough trusts the value is a scalar. Worth a low-severity note.

Let me verify there's no separate serializer leaking via RunRead and double-check the `dict[str, Any]` typing allows a non-dict sample (since gx could in theory put a list). Backend `redact_sample_failures` signature is `dict | None`, and the dict comprehension assumes `.items()`. The DB column is JSONB and `_extract_sample_failures` always returns a dict or None, so fine.

Let me check one more: does the retention purge set `sample_failures` to SQL null, and does `_result_read` handle that? `redact_sample_failures(None)` returns None. Good.

I've completed a thorough review. The redaction boundary is sound. Findings:

```json
[
  {
    "file": "backend/app/services/run_service.py",

    "summary": "_SAMPLE_SAFE_KEYS passthrough trusts the value is a scalar aggregate; it copies unexpected_count/unexpected_percent verbatim regardless of type.",
    "failure_scenario": "Today gx_runner only ever puts GX-computed scalars under these keys (_SAMPLE_KEYS extraction from GX's own result), so this is safe. But the redactor is the security boundary and it special-cases by KEY NAME, not value shape. If any future runner/expectation (e.g. a custom-SQL or DQX path) ever populates sample_failures with a non-scalar under 'unexpected_count'/'unexpected_percent' (a list/dict of row data), it bypasses redaction entirely and ships raw. Hardening: pass these keys through only when isinstance(value, (int, float)); otherwise fall through to _redact_sample_value. Low severity now, no current leak."
  },
  {
    "file": "frontend/src/pages/RunDetail.tsx",
    "line": 240,
    "summary": "String(v) on a non-scalar redacted cell renders '[object Object]' / mangled output, not a value.",
    "failure_scenario": "If a redacted row entry is a nested dict/array (backend masks leaves but preserves container shape, e.g. {addr:{city:'<redacted>'}}), the column render does String({city:'<redacted>'}) = '[object Object]'. Not a PII leak (values already masked), but a display/correctness wart. Cosmetic only."
  }
]
```

## Summary

I reviewed the full diff plus the originals (`run_service.py`, `runs.py`, `gx_runner.py`, `probe.py`, `RunDetail.tsx`). **No real PII leak path found.** The redaction boundary is well-constructed:

**Leak paths checked and cleared:**
- **Construction sites:** `_result_read()` is the only path building `ResultRead` with `sample_failures`. The detail endpoint (`get_run`) routes results through it. `list_runs` returns `RunRead` (no results/sample_failures field). The probe endpoint's `CheckResultResponse` does not declare `sample_failures`, so Pydantic never serializes it. No `model_validate(from_attributes)` path auto-copies the raw column anywhere.
- **Nested "safe key" bypass:** `_SAMPLE_SAFE_KEYS` is only honored at the top level (the dict comprehension checks `key in _SAMPLE_SAFE_KEYS` once). Row dicts nested under `partial_unexpected_list` go through `_redact_sample_value`, which masks every leaf regardless of key name — so a column literally named `unexpected_count` is still masked. Confirmed by the test `test_redact_sample_failures_masks_unknown_keys_and_nested_values`.
- **Unknown keys:** any key other than the two safe ones is fully masked (verified against `unexpected_index_list` test). Non-dict/non-list values become `<redacted>`.
- **gx_runner source shape:** `_SAMPLE_KEYS = (partial_unexpected_list, unexpected_count, unexpected_percent)`. The two passthrough keys are GX-computed aggregates, never column/row data and not data-controlled.
- **None handling:** purged/passing results (`sample_failures = NULL`) pass through as `None`.

**Correctness:** pluralization (`count === 1`) and percent/count rendering are correct; the scalar-vs-dict entry normalization is sound; empty-list redacts to empty list.

**Two low-severity notes** (neither a current leak): the safe-key passthrough trusts value type by key name rather than shape (latent fragility if a future runner misuses those keys), and `String(v)` on a nested redacted object renders `[object Object]` (cosmetic only).

346	# ── sample-failures redaction (PII-safe surfacing on the read API) ────────────
347	
348	# Aggregate summary keys in a GX sample are counts/percentages, not row data, so
349	# they are safe to surface. Everything else — notably `partial_unexpected_list`,
350	# the raw offending cell values — is treated as potential PII and masked.
351	_SAMPLE_SAFE_KEYS = frozenset({"unexpected_count", "unexpected_percent"})
352	_REDACTED_VALUE = "<redacted>"
353	
354	
355	def _redact_sample_value(value: Any) -> Any:
356	    """Mask data values while preserving container shape and dict keys.
357	
358	    List length and row-dict column names are *schema*, not row data, so they
359	    stay (they tell the viewer how many rows / which columns failed); every leaf
360	    value is replaced with ``"<redacted>"``.
361	    """
362	    if isinstance(value, dict):
363	        return {key: _redact_sample_value(val) for key, val in value.items()}
364	    if isinstance(value, list):
365	        return [_redact_sample_value(item) for item in value]
366	    return _REDACTED_VALUE
367	
368	
369	def redact_sample_failures(sample: dict[str, Any] | None) -> dict[str, Any] | None:
370	    """Redact a result's `sample_failures` for safe surfacing on the read API.
371	
372	    `sample_failures` carries aggregate counts plus `partial_unexpected_list` —
373	    the actual failing cell values, which can be PII. Suite-level ``view`` authz
374	    lets share-recipients read a suite's results, so the raw rows must not cross
375	    that boundary unredacted (CLAUDE.md PII rule; the column is redactor-only
376	    everywhere else and is purged on the retention sweep below).
377	
378	    Policy: keep the numeric summary keys; mask every value in the rest,
379	    preserving shape (list length, row-dict column names) so the drill-down shows
380	    *how much* failed without leaking *what*. ``None`` (passing / errored /
381	    purged results) passes through unchanged. A future opt-in could surface raw
382	    rows to privileged viewers — that policy seam lives at this one call site.
383	    """
384	    if not sample:
385	        return None
386	    return {
387	        key: (value if key in _SAMPLE_SAFE_KEYS else _redact_sample_value(value))
388	        for key, value in sample.items()
389	    }
390	
391	
392	# ── retention sweep (configurable PII purge of old result samples) ────────────
393

> TOOL

id: toolu_01RX3SdFQe9FqHaLZmAzXVEV
```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/arijit/Coding/Python/DataQ/backend/app/services/run_service.py",
  "old_string": "    Policy: keep the numeric summary keys; mask every value in the rest,\n    preserving shape (list length, row-dict column names) so the drill-down shows\n    *how much* failed without leaking *what*. ``None`` (passing / errored /\n    purged results) passes through unchanged. A future opt-in could surface raw\n    rows to privileged viewers — that policy seam lives at this one call site.\n    \"\"\"\n    if not sample:\n        return None\n    return {\n        key: (value if key in _SAMPLE_SAFE_KEYS else _redact_sample_value(value))\n        for key, value in sample.items()\n    }",
  "new_string": "    Policy: keep the numeric summary keys; mask every value in the rest,\n    preserving shape (list length, row-dict column names) so the drill-down shows\n    *how much* failed without leaking *what*. ``None`` (passing / errored /\n    purged results) passes through unchanged. A future opt-in could surface raw\n    rows to privileged viewers — that policy seam lives at this one call site.\n\n    This redacts by *value* (mask everything except an allowlist), not by *key*\n    like the structlog redactor (``core.logging._PII_KEYS``): a failing sample is\n    arbitrary table columns whose PII-ness can't be enumerated as key names ahead\n    of time, so masking only known-PII keys would leak any un-enumerated column.\n    Do not \"harmonise\" this toward the key-based approach.\n\n    A safe key is passed through only when its value is actually a scalar number —\n    the boundary trusts value *shape*, not just the key name — so a future runner\n    that ever stowed row data under one of those keys can't bypass redaction.\n    \"\"\"\n    if not sample:\n        return None\n    return {\n        key: (value if _is_safe_summary(key, value) else _redact_sample_value(value))\n        for key, value in sample.items()\n    }\n\n\ndef _is_safe_summary(key: str, value: Any) -> bool:\n    \"\"\"A passthrough-safe aggregate: an allowlisted key whose value is a plain\n    number (``bool`` excluded — it's an ``int`` subclass but not a count).\"\"\"\n    return key in _SAMPLE_SAFE_KEYS and isinstance(value, (int, float)) and not isinstance(value, bool)"
}

{

'' : String(v)}\n      </Typography.Text>\n    ),\n  }));",
  "new_string": "  // GX's partial_unexpected_list rows share one schema, but union the keys\n  // defensively so a ragged sample still renders every column.\n  const colKeys = [...new Set(rows.flatMap((r) => Object.keys(r)))];\n  const columns: ColumnsType<Record<string, unknown>> = colKeys.map((key) => ({\n    title: key,\n    dataIndex: key,\n    render: (v: unknown) => (\n      <Typography.Text type=\"secondary\" style={{ fontSize: 12 }}>\n        {/* Values are already masked by the API; stringify objects so a nested\n            redacted cell shows as JSON rather than \"[object Object]\". */}\n        {v === undefined ? '' : typeof v === 'object' && v !== null ? JSON.stringify(v) : String(v)}\n      </Typography.Text>\n    ),\n  }));"
}
